NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, August 12, 2014 
 
PRESENT: Jonathan M. Nez, Supervisor; Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David 
Tenney, Vice Chairman; and Jesse Thompson, Chairman were present.  
9:00 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Nez.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments.  
      CONSENT AGENDA : a) Minutes: July 22, 2014 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 July 2014 c) 
Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, June &  July; and Show Low Precinct #5, July, 2014 d) Tax 
exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42 -11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver e) Adoption of the 
Proposed FY2014 -15 Cost Allocation Schedule f) Intergovernmental Agreement among Pima, Maricopa, 
Yavapai, Gila and Navajo County School Superintendents to establish the Arizona Education and Technology 
Consortium g) Application for State funding for the Field Trainer Grant for FY15 h) Certify revenue collected 
by Superior Court, Clerk of the Court and all six Justice Courts i) Reissuance of stale dated checks to various 
taxpayers and investors for overpayment of taxes and CP redemptions j) Contract Amendment #DE14 -051529, 
Amendment #1 for Federal Funds awarded to the Navajo and Apache County Workforce Partn ership k) Local 
Workforce Investment Board By Laws l) Contracts signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors 
Resolution: Frontier Communications Services Agreements with the Navajo County Library District for the 
following service locations: Snowflake , Woodruff, Heber, Clay Springs and Holbrook; and Contract 
Amendment #2, Contract for Professional Services with Roman Bitsui m) Contract Amendment for Levi, Ray 
& Shoup, Inc. in the amount of $5,000 for additional services for website redesign project n) Installation of a 
STOP sign on Tumbleweed Trail and the intersection with Outlaw Pass o) Sympathy Letter to the family of 
Senator Chester Crandell : Supervisor Tenney made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in 
the consent agenda as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS : Presentation to Sergeant William Murray and Deputy Jim Watson for their 
efforts in removing 68 pounds of dangerous drugs from Navajo County, making th is a better community for all: 
Chief Jim Molesa recognized Sergeant William Murray and Deputy Jim Watson for their efforts in removing 68 
pounds of methamphetamines traveling through Navajo County for the month of June. Chief Molesa indicated 
that their hard work removed 704,256 dosage units of crystal methamphetamine from going to individual users . 
Chief Molesa indicated that i f we can save one person from addiction , than we are making an impact in  saving 
lives. Chief Molesa introduced  the drug dogs,  Russell and Flores . Chairman Thompson recognized Sergeant 
Murray and Deputy Watson with a certificate and plaque of appreciation for making this a better community for 
all.  
WE ARE NAVAJO COUNTY : Presentation of the CARE Committee’s Back to School Back Pack Drive: 
Kimberly Eavenson gave a presentation of the CARE Committee’s Back to School B ack Pack Drive. Mrs. 
Eavenson indicated t here were 45  children from 16 Navajo County families  who received back packs. Mrs. 
Eavenson thanked Anna Erickson and the Assessor ’s office team for their work on this project in collecting the 
backpacks and school supplies. James Jayne thanked Assessor Cammy Darris, Darlene Fraley  and Anna 
Erickson for their leadership.  Mr. Jayne spoke of the CARE Co mmittee and their service in providing help to  
the Navajo County employees and their families.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-12-2014 
    Page 2 of 5 
 
 
Supervisor Tenney invited everyone to sing Happy Birthday to Supervisor Whitesinger.  
HUMAN RESOURCES:  
a. Personnel Service Awards : Congratulations to the following employees for their continued service to 
Navajo County: Debbie Serna (County Attorney’s Office) 15 years and Gregory Kroeger (Sheriff’s 
Office) 20 years: Eric Scott recognized Debbie Serna  for her 15 years of service to Nav ajo County and 
Gregory Kroeger for his 20 years of service to Navajo County.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson reviewed the 45 item 
personnel action report. Supervisor Tenney  made a motion to approve  the personnel actions as 
presented; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger . Supervisor Allen  declared a conflict  of 
interest. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible approval to appoint Wade Carlisle and David Coolidge to serve  on the 
Disciplinary Appeals Review Commission, to each serve a term of four years : Kimberly Eavenson 
discussed the item  and indicated that five persons are to serve on the Disciplinary A ppeals Review 
Commission and that we currently have two vacant positi ons. Mrs. Eavenson asked the Board of 
Supervisors to appoint David Coolidge to serve ou t the term of Harold Ni mitz who passed away, and to 
appoint Wade Carlisle  to replace Carol Lewis whose term expired May 25, 2014 . Supervisor Allen  
inquired about the three individuals currently on the commission now. Mrs. Eavenson indicated that 
Brian Shields, LaR ee Price and Blaine Hatch  are presently serving on the commission . Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion  to appoint Wade Carlisle and David Coolidge to serve on the  Disciplinary 
Appeals Review Commission, to each serve a term of four years ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      ASSESSOR: Consideration and possible approval of Pictometry Contract #35F0801N, for aerial imagery  of 
specified areas of Navajo County : Darlene Fraley , Deputy Assessor  gave a presentation of the Pictometry 
Contract #35F 0801N, for aerial imagery of specified are as of Navajo County. Mrs. Fraley explained the 
advantages of using the aerial imagery for improved accuracy. She stated this information will only be available 
to the Navajo County employees for use  and it will not be on the web . Supervisor Allen made a motion to 
approve the Pictometry Contract #35F0801N, for aerial imagery of specified areas of Navajo County; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY ATTORNEY/SHERIFF:  
a. Recognition of Sara Wolfe, APAAC 2014 Juvenile Attorney of the Year : Brad Carlyon , County 
Attorney recognized Sara Wolfe for  receiving the award from the Arizona Prosecuting Attorneys 
Advisory Council, 2014 Juvenile Attorney of the Year. Mr. Carlyon read the letter  of congratulations to 
Mrs. Wolfe, from the Board of Supervisors. Chairman Thompson thanked Sara Wolfe for her excellent 
communication skills with all the individuals she works with. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Mrs. 
Wolfe for setting the standard for the  young people in our community. Supervisor Allen expressed 
appreciation to Mrs. Wolfe for being a great role model for our young generation. Mr. Carlyon indicated 
that many other attorneys in the Navajo Cou nty Attorney’s office have been recognized statewide and 
nationally. 
b. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number  19-14, opposing the Legalization of 
Marijuana: Brad Carlyon and Chief Jim Molesa gave a presentation on the  2016 ballot measure of 
legalizing marijuana. Mr. Carlyon and Chief Molesa answered questio ns of the Board of Supervisors. 
Supervisor Allen thanked the County Attorney and the Sheriff’s office for brin ging this resolution to 
the Board. Supervisor Allen  asked for the presentation , so that she can share at her t own halls,

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-12-2014 
    Page 3 of 5 
 
 
“Marijuana Harmless, Think A gain, which  is the statewide movement ”. Supervisor Tenney made a 
motion to adopt Resolution Number 19-14, opposing the Legalization of Marijuana ; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
TREASURER: Consideration and possible approval of Annual Report of collections, delinquencies and 
settlement of accounts : Manny Hernandez, Treasurer discussed the item and answered questions of the Board. 
Mr. Hernandez reported on the taxes collected and investments. Mr. Hernandez thanked his staff for doing more 
with less.  
ADMINISTRATION:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Show 
Low and Navajo County regarding joint use of a gym and workout area for all employees and their 
family members: Brandt Clark discussed the item indicating that all county employees and their families 
will be able t o utilize this City of Show Low gym  along with the City of Show Low employees . 
Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve  the Memorandum of Understanding between the City 
of Show Low and Navajo County regarding joint use of a gym and workout area for all e mployees and 
their family members ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. Mr. Jayne thanked Brandt Clark for his work on the agreement. 
b. Consideration and possible approval to donate a used color copy machine to Nava jo County Fair, Inc. : 
Paige Peterson discussed the item. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve to donate a used 
color copy machine to Navajo County Fair, Inc. ; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote  
unanimous approving the motion.  
PUBLIC WORKS: Consideration and possible approval to rescind the award of contract B14-05-028 Excavate 
and Crush Red Rock Material at the Peabody Western Coal Company with Fann Contracting Inc. and award to 
Perkins Cinders, Inc. : Homero Vela discussed the item. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve to 
rescind the award of contract B14-05-028 Excavate and Crush Red Rock Material at the Peabody Western Coal 
Company with Fann Contracting Inc. and award to Perkins Cinders, Inc. ; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS /FINANCE: Consideration and possible adoption of the FY2014 -15 
property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within Navajo County : Linda Morrow, 
Superintendent of Schools discussed the Navajo County public s chool district property tax rates  and answered 
questions of the Board. She spoke of the school tax rates and indicated that the schools are do ing a great job of 
keeping tax rates as low as possible  for the taxpayers of the county. Karen MacKean, a resident of Show Low, 
a property owner, and an employee of  Americans for Prosperity,  spoke to the Board  regarding the county 
property tax increases  which she indicated affects all of us. Ms. MacKean  asked about the PILT  funds in 
relation to the decision to increase taxes . Ms. MacKean inquired that if the PILT funds are received , will the 
property tax payers be reimbursed , or will this decision be reversed ? Supervisor Tenney indicated that once 
property tax rates are set and collected there would not be a way to lower the rate s, or the taxpayers to be 
reimbursed. He stated that in subsequent years the tax rates could be changed. Supervisor Tenney indicated he 
is working for a permanent solution for PILT to be funded, in w hich he is speaking with our legislators. Steven 
Slaton, a resident of Snowflake , asked the Board to vote no on the tax increase. Mr. Slaton stated  most 
residents live from paycheck to paycheck, and most people and small business es will be hurt by the tax 
increases and cannot afford this, especially because most have not received pay increases. Chairman 
Thompson indicated that this Board has taken this into consideration for a long time . He stated the Board

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-12-2014 
    Page 4 of 5 
 
 
takes into account the different ways to provide s ervices to the public.  Linda Morrow indicated that the area 
schools have worked very hard to  keep the tax rates down. M rs. Morrow reported that the schools did a good 
job in continuing to keep a high level of quality service to their students and communities, with less money and 
less support. She stated the school tax rates for this coming year , have been set and adopted by the elected  
school board members from each school district. Mrs. Morrow indicated the rates were  set by those boards  at 
open meetings, and the meetings are published on their websites.  Mrs. Morrow indicated she is presenting 
these to the Board for their approval.  Supervisor Allen made a motion  to adopt the fiscal year 2014 -15 
Navajo County public school district property tax rates; motio n seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. Linda Morrow thanked the Board and left the meeting. James Menlove gave 
a summary of the Tax Levies & Rates for non -school entities throughout the county. Mr. Menlove indicated 
this includes tax-rates for Navajo County itself, Navajo County Library District, Navajo County Public Health 
Services District, Flood Control Districts and Road Districts. The other entities are Fire Districts, Sanitary 
Districts, Water Improvement District, Cities and Towns and Northland Pioneer Community College. He 
indicated these entities have their own boards and have adopted their own budgets , which they send to the 
County for their levies and tax rates. Supervisor Nez made a motion to adopt the Navajo County summary of 
Tax Levie s and Rates for Fiscal Year 2014 -15; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Supervisor Nez , 
Supervisor Tenney , Supervisor Whitesinger  and Chairman Thompson  voted yeah. Supervisor Allen  
voted in opposition. Motion carried 4 to 1. 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Nez reported he attended  Senator Flake’s visit to Show Low. H e spoke of District I ’s efforts to 
create a Fire District in the Kayenta  and Pinon region. Supervisor Nez indicated the Peabody Western Coal 
Company is a contributor of taxes to the county along with the Navajo Generating Station in Coconino County. 
He indicated the constituents  hope to tap into those resources to get the process started.  Supervisor N ez 
indicated there were  17 candid ates running for the Navajo Nation P residency, along with other candidates  
running for the statewide districts. He reminded everyone about the primary election that will be held on 
August 26  and encouraged all to vote .  He reported on an initiative that was passed by the Navajo Nation 
Council, the opposing of the legalization of marijuana. Supervisor Nez thanked the county for their help with 
the 4th annual “Running in Beauty  for a stronger  Navajo Nation”.  He indicated it was over 400 miles. 
Supervisor Nez indicated he ran through the Hopi 60 road , with President Honanie from the Hopi Tribe . He 
further spoke of the graveling projects in his district and the partnership with the Navajo Nation and the BIA. 
He sent his condolences to  the Crandell family, for the loss of the late Senator  Chester Crandell. Supervisor 
Allen spoke of Senator Crandell’s passing and sent her condolences to his family. She further thanked Homero 
Vela and Melissa Buckley for their help with the Joseph City Walking Path Ribbon Cutting.  Supervisor Allen 
spoke of the Public Hearing  for the Mexican Wolf. She indicated that many of the suggestions of the 27 
agencies, including Navajo County were not heard in regards to the MOU of the releas e of the Mexican Wolfs. 
Supervisor Allen announced that her name is in the running to replace Senator Chester Crandell on the ballot. 
Chairman Thompson reported on the milling project for different roads in his District. Chairman Thompson 
left the meeting at 11:21 a .m. Supervisor Whitesinger  reported on a committee that she serves  on for the 
White Mountain Apache Tribal communities on depression  and alcoholism. She indicated the committee is 
partnering with the community to provide services for those w ho have substance abuse issues. Supervisor 
Whitesinger indicated she is on a committee for Books for Arizona Kids. She stated she is a Board member of 
the First Things First for the Tribal Community. She stated she attended the meeting with Senator Flake where 
there were discussion s about the issues that affect her  communities. Supervisor Tenney offered his 
condolences to the family of Senator Crandell. He reported on the meeting with Senator Flake  in Show Low 
and the Senator’s assistance with getting funding for the forests. He reported on the movement with the Forest

<!-- page 5 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-12-2014 
    Page 5 of 5 
 
 
Service to receive funds for the treatment of forests . He indicated he met with President Biggs regarding 
gaining support for the HURF dollars and other  funds for county assistance. Supervisor Tenney indicated it 
has been two years since the passing of his father. He stated he attended the 50th wedding anniversary of Mr. & 
Mrs. Rick Allen. He extended his support to Supervisor Allen  for the Senate seat. Brad Carlyon reminded 
everyone to send in  their ear ly ballots and to vote in the election. Mr. Carlyon spoke about the Family 
Advocacy Center’s Fashion Show for fundraising, and indicated the Family Advocacy Center’s Facebook page 
has photos of the event. He stated that the fundraiser is for a go od cause. James Jayne indicated this Friday, 
Hunter Moore and Navajo County will be hosting a meeting of the 15 , Arizona  County Man agers, for the 
County Managers Association in Show Low. 
      ADJOURN: At 11:33 a.m. Supervisor Nez  made a motion to adjourn ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
DATE:  August 26, 2014 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
